Shared native event feed recovery (11 November 2026): user reported A53 could neither find peers nor be found after the latest installation. Wired diagnostics on PID 13442 confirmed Bluetooth on, BLE scan/connect/advertise permissions granted and native service-filtered scan callbacks from two locators, while Flutter logged no deviceFound events and the Home screenshot showed an empty list. Native advertising succeeded initially, then disposal/reset events occurred; subsequent discovery callbacks did not reach the app. This is evidence of a software event-delivery fault, not hardware failure diagnosis. Reproduced an owner-transport overlap in a host regression: a new BleTransport subscribed to the same EventChannel, then the old transport disposed; deviceFound no longer reached the new owner (test failed before correction). BleTransport now shares the raw native broadcast stream per BinaryMessenger/channel name via an Expando, while each owner retains its own readiness cache, connection generation matcher and relay proof state. Cancelling the previous owner cannot cancel the current owner's native event sink. Last transport disposal still cancels the native subscription. Focused transport/presence/UI/lifecycle/coordinator/recovery suites passed 78/78; app/test analysis and diff checks passed. Built fresh Android arm64 Profile and installed in place on USB A53 RFCT80GPFVY; quick_check passed and the entire DB remained byte-identical with 30 SOS, 131 Chat and 37 rescue inbox rows. No history was cleared and no hardware reset was performed.

Authenticated SOS deletion/restore correction (11 November 2026): user reported Delete success toast while logged-in history retained the SOS. Source identified that repository deletion removed local row and legacy /sos document, but CloudArchiveService immediately restored the surviving accounts/{owner}/archiveSos document during sync. Deletion now supersedes older queued edits and removes the owner-scoped archive parent plus legacy document before acknowledgement. Failed deletion stays durably queued. After acknowledgement, the existing sosSyncQueue retains an internal deleteConfirmed marker, excluded from future upload attempts; no DB schema migration is needed. Archive upload/restore checks pending/confirmed deletion intent by owner+incident, with transactional checks before SOS/Chat restore commits, so a stale in-flight archive response cannot recreate a locally deleted SOS. Deleted-incident Chat metadata is not re-uploaded/restored; existing local Chat/media and remote orphan Chat subcollections are not purged by this change. This is not a cross-device remote tombstone protocol: another installation with old local data can still republish its archive, while this installation retains its local deletion marker. Existing checked-in archive rules allow owner delete; no rules/Storage change or deployment made. New production-repository/archive regression tests cover stale restore after confirmed deletion, failed archive delete/retry, older create supersession and cross-owner isolation. Focused repository/sync/history/navigation/nearby suites passed 67/67. Earlier full-suite failures remain distinct. iOS build/preflight/installation and physical delete acceptance are pending at this entry; no user incident was deleted by the agent.

Latest implementation increment — retained-route duplicate recovery (10 November 2026): duplicate selection prioritizes a proof-complete, non-retiring session over a new declaration; deterministic key ordering remains for concurrent unproven sessions. Optional BleCentralRecovery.retireCentralLink is implemented in Android/iOS native bridges so rejecting an outgoing duplicate does not explicitly clear inbound subscriptions/queues as generic disconnect did. Existing legacy transport fallback remains conservative. Bounded native locator/key hints are also learned after verification of a session-bound signed challenge, including a redundant alias rejected before reciprocal proof completes; unsigned advertisements do not populate that map or authorize traffic. Known aliases suppress initiation only while the same installation retains a live proven route, releasing recovery when that route is lost. _challengeDeclaredPeers uses a snapshot and skips replaced entries across awaits. Focused suites passed 150/150, final selector suite 38/38 and restored targeted regressions 2/2; analysis/diff checks passed. Negative checks temporarily restored the old selection/live-iteration conditions: both new regressions failed, including Concurrent modification; fixes were restored before builds. Simulation verifies retained inbound SOS/receipt delivery after rejecting a preferred outgoing duplicate, authenticated alias retry suppression/release and disconnect during proof refresh. Android Profile APK built; iOS build underway. Not installed or physically verified. Other native GATT failure causes and picker Unknown model/alias merging remain unresolved. Detailed physical evidence remains at the end of this file.

Selected-installation alias reselection regression (11 November 2026): review extended the recovery simulation to reselect the old picker alias while the new authenticated route remained alive, then send another Chat and require its final receipt. The prior draft failed this new check by quarantining the active sibling alias. Explicit reuse of a proven installation now retains the currently proven route and activates that exact route instead of revoking the sibling; explicit reselection without a proven route can still authorize a replacement key. The targeted check now passes, with final focused/security suites 222/222 and analysis clean. Native recovery hint caches are also cleared on dispose (not on ordinary adapter recovery), preventing stale owner/coordinator metadata carryover. A03s received an earlier in-place draft with its 13 SOS/25 Chat/19 rescue rows byte-identical; all devices still await the final corrected artifact and physical auto-recovery acceptance. Final native rebuild underway.

Selected-installation recovery final validation (11 November 2026): all focused policy, production simulation, relay/repository, lifecycle, transport, Chat runtime/security and native-confirmed source-contract suites passed 221/221. Analysis and diff checks passed. Production advertisement codecs executed on JVM and Swift host runtimes passed new/legacy/malformed packet and size-budget checks. Final Android arm64 Profile and signed iOS Profile builds passed, including strict signature and standalone preflight. The runtime simulation preserves the same recipient database/key across a new native locator and confirms queued Chat plus final receipt without manual reselection; a cloned hint cannot receive incident data or inject a Direct SOS before proof, and rejecting it does not revoke the true selected installation. Old-locator key substitution is denied, while explicit manual reselection can authorize a new key. A03s wired installation is underway; no physical automatic-recovery result is claimed yet.

Selected-installation recovery implementation (11 November 2026): operator confirmed final SOS cancellation removed A53 Helping entry while retaining History/Chat. Following verified A53 native-locator rotation, manual selection now remembers the fully proof-verified installation key for the current owner/coordinator lifetime. Added an optional public 32-bit discovery hint derived from that key: Android versioned 13-byte service-data scan response (marker 0xA7, eight model bytes, four hint bytes; 18+13=31-byte budget), and iOS compact R:hint:M:model local name. Both native scanners parse new/legacy metadata and emit recoveryHint; old transport/service UUIDs and signed message formats remain unchanged. Hint publication is prepared before relay advertising; cached native metadata is applied on advertising start without restarting active GATT links. Foreground name/scan-response availability is required; no new background guarantee is made. Hints are collision-prone, public and spoofable candidate filters only. New locators are quarantined: only relay capability/challenge/response messages can pass; no Direct binding, SOS, lifecycle, Chat, location, media or history replay is enabled before proof of the exact selected key. Full-key policy is checked both on declaration and before proof commit, including automatic retry of an old selected locator. Rejection leaves candidate data blocked and permits recovery of the real device; ambiguous selected-key hint collisions require manual choice. Proof success migrates selection to the verified locator and suppresses retries beside any existing proven route. Explicit disconnect revokes all known aliases/candidates of that selected key; explicit manual reselection can authorize a replacement installation. State is in-memory and owner scoped, without DB migration or cloud writes. Initial focused/security suites passed 219/219; extra old-locator substitution/manual-reselection checks passed with all recovery-policy/runtime tests 11/11. Host JVM/Swift execution of production metadata codecs and packet-budget checks passed; native artifact builds prepared before the final policy tightening, so final rebuild/validation still pending. Physical auto-reconnect remains unverified.

Confirmed TX manual recovery proves locator rotation (11 November 2026): operator selected the currently discovered A53 once from A03s, without recreating SOS or resending Chat, and reported one arrival plus iPhone recipient confirmation. Read-only A03s SQLite quick_check passed; joining the same message ID confirms delivered with retry_count one and updatedAt 00:29:03. Native logs show the new outgoing locator selected at 00:28:51, confirmed_indications ready at 00:28:53 and Session Proof at 00:29:03. Its authenticated key matches the initial A53 session's key while its native locator differs. This verifies locator rotation across this A53 adapter restart and successful same-message recovery after manual reselection. Automatic selected-peer continuity remains broken because production initiation consent/retry is keyed to native locators; current verified-locator reuse prevents redundant known aliases but cannot discover the identity of an unseen alias. No automatic trust by model/name should be introduced: any future discovery hint is only a candidate filter and must be checked against the previously selected signing key before incident/history/Chat data can be sent. A stable discovery-hint plus authenticated selected-installation recovery design is required; not implemented in this diagnostic turn. Preserve this distinction from the passed foreground SOS/bidirectional Chat/manual recovery tests.

Confirmed TX native flow-control increment (overnight 10–11 November 2026): cross-device trace correlation found A03s locally submitting 43 notification frames during 23:54:33 while iPhone Dart observed approximately 3–5 frames/second and continued receiving through 23:54:47. This demonstrates observable producer/consumer backlog, not a definitive location of buffering inside radio, OS or app. A03s logged upstream ATT writes through request 28 at 23:54:39.580, but the old bridge did not log sendResponse return values, so that capture cannot prove ATT response delivery or the next missing write's exact location. Added a separate Android indication-only CONFIRMED_TX characteristic (8b7f0004-6d3a-4c8b-9d21-5a7f2e110001); legacy notification TX and service/RX UUIDs remain available. Updated Android/iOS centrals prefer the new characteristic, validate its indication property and subscribe accordingly, falling back to legacy TX only when the new characteristic is absent. Android server selects notify/indicate per subscribed client, requests confirmation for the new channel, and retains one in-flight frame/timeout per client; mode-switch attempts during an active subscription are rejected. Connection events explicitly report confirmed_indications or legacy_notifications, including cached/legacy discovery fallback. ATT response/CCCD replies now precede Flutter data/readiness publication and their local acceptance is logged. This is BLE-level confirmation, never SOS persistence or final-recipient confirmation. Dart wire envelopes/signatures/storage remain unchanged. Three native source-contract tests and analysis passed; Android Profile and signed iOS Profile compilation, signature and standalone preflight passed. These checks do not simulate actual ATT delivery; flow-control benefit, GATT-cache behavior and three-device stability remain unverified. A03s wired installation is underway; A53/iPhone need the corresponding native update before physical testing.

Subsequent attempts 2–5 failed GATT setup at approximately 16-second intervals. Incoming old lifecycle frames continued during the first write stall. The previous flood of repeated failure logs was reduced, but the real native callback/GATT recovery failure persists, so this is not a passed stability test. Retained iPhone syslog did not include Swift NSLog traces, limiting the native write/response detail. Need A03s's 23:54–23:56 ATT request/disconnect trace before attributing the timeout to missing reception, server response, central callback, concurrent radio load, or another native condition. No additional implementation change was made from this failed-test capture.

Lifecycle replay final validation and A53 pre-update capture (10 November 2026): added an opaque native connection token to relay sessions, preserved across proof-generation refresh and replaced on native reconnect. Relay control/data writes now provide the same strict connection-current guard as lifecycle replay, so a late capability/proof timeout cannot disconnect replacement queued work. This remains separate from consent/session canSend cancellation. Final focused tests passed 168/168 and analysis passed; Android arm64 Profile and signed iOS Profile builds passed, including strict signature/standalone preflight. Pre-update A53 SQLite quick_check passed and contained no relay message row at capture. Its current-process log had zero outgoing connect requests, seven authenticated sessions with one peer key distinct from the iPhone origin, and an inbound link_probe_timeout at 23:24:16 followed by resumed inbound sessions from 23:33 onward. This differs from the prior reverse-connect loop but does not prove radio stability. Scan observations appeared 23:26:33–23:27:03 with no scan failure callback; the cause of disappearing discovery rows remains unverified. A53 wired installation of the replay/session correction is in progress; A03s and iPhone remain on the preceding retained-route artifact.

Lifecycle replay/session recovery increment (10 November 2026): read-only wired A03s snapshot passed quick_check and retained the 21:48:42 SOS with renewed expiry 23:51:31; no outgoing relay attempt was present in that capture. Its logs correlate the iPhone disconnect at 23:25:37 and proof at 23:25:42, while the other central link repeatedly times out during GATT setup. This separates completed first-hop storage from the still-unresolved second-hop setup failure. SosBleCoordinator now registers per-native-role connection tokens before awaiting relay authentication and rejects a delayed connected handler after that token was retired. Lifecycle history replay is coalesced per live connection, sends each event at most once on that connection after native completion, stops on connection change/first write failure, and retains durable rows for reconnect replay. Immediate lifecycle forwarding shares the same replay path; repeated readiness/binding requests do not independently flood history. Adapter loss/stop retire tokens. BleMessageWriteQueue accepts an optional isConnectionCurrent guard distinct from canSend: a timeout from a retired connection fails only that old write and does not disconnect/fail fresh queued work, while active-connection timeout recovery remains unchanged. Tests passed 167/167 and analysis/diff checks passed. Regressions cover replay dedup, failure then reconnect delivery, stale callback ordering and old-timeout/new-queue isolation. Android/iOS artifact builds underway, not installed. These changes address proven application recovery hazards; remaining native GATT setup instability, unknown-model rows and A53 discovery disappearance are not declared fixed.

Retained-route physical retest and iPhone capture (10 November 2026): operator reported A53 Unknown model/Disconnect, A03s alternating iPhone Connect/Disconnect and A53 Connecting, iPhone alternating A03s Connect/Disconnect, then nearby entries disappeared on A53. This fails the requested stable-link acceptance. Read-only wired iPhone SQLite quick_check passed; the original 21:48:42 incident had renewed expiry 23:51:31 and a first-hop acceptedByRelay attempt at 23:23:59 (retry count three). Fresh live Runner logs showed native readiness, successful Session Proof, and numerous old lifecycle replay failures immediately before a central disconnect at 23:25:37, including eight-second native call timeouts and subsequent Peripheral notification session is not ready errors. Another proof completed at 23:25:42. Source review found lifecycle replay is triggered before/after connected handling and after binding, has no per-peer coalescing/dedup for that live connection, and continues over history after write failures with a canSend guard that does not check connection/session freshness. This is an additional concrete recovery/backlog concern, not proof that it caused every native timeout or the A53 discovery disappearance. Current A03s trace is needed to correlate the first-hop failure and pending second hop; no code change or device restart was performed in this diagnostic turn.
